Minecraft seed searcher — current project packet (11 October 2026)

GitHub: https://github.com/lucasraicu/minecraft-seed-searcher
Base commit: e3a5ca556e93f79df77b5cfc696d0226c8437c49
This packet includes LOCAL UNCOMMITTED terrain-preference changes after that commit.

Current authoritative settings:
- Bedrock 26.52, pinned approximate MC_26_50 predictor; BDS 1.26.52.3.
- Discovery: 4000 x 4000 in Overworld and initial Nether.
- Final Overworld: 1536 x 1536 centered on each discovered Stronghold.
- Final Nether: 896 x 896 at the exact fractional Stronghold/8 center.
- Required: Stronghold, Village, Trial Chamber, Ancient City, Mineshaft;
  initial Fortress; final Fortress AND Bastion; at least two wooded families.
- Independent full64 search is the default. Lower32 is experimental.
- Current main-island preference: at least 250,000 connected land blocks,
  favor 650,000; disconnected islets do not add to the main island area.
  Irregular shapes are allowed; equivalent diameter is an area summary.
- Basin preference: substantial raised terrain around the interior, allowing
  separate mountains, rough/water interiors and open passes. No strict arc shape
  in the active surrounding_mountains policy.

Evidence:
- 680 tests passed after the terrain preference update.
- Compact eligibility pilots: 603–612 inputs/sec INCLUDING persistence,
  EXCLUDING deferred inventory, terrain and BDS. These precede the latest
  terrain preference changes; no new sustained terrain/BDS rate is measured.
- Equivalent frozen-lattice speedup: 1.53x. Local mineshaft witness speedup ~48x.
- Prior massive benchmark: 173.37/s at OLDER 5000/2688/1024 geometry.
- Lower32 necessary raw predicate rejected 3.895% in the larger compact pilot;
  persisted presence ~1.44 million layouts/sec, not useful full-world yield.
- New 2048-layout pilot: Monument/Outpost/Shipwreck/Ruined Portal added zero
  envelope-presence exclusions; at one arbitrary center Outpost added 38.
  This is not an actual Stronghold or all-family joint-center experiment.
- Predictor parity is not actual-world accuracy. Full structure recall and
  whole natural formation verification remain open; no production sweep launched.

Read config/realm.yaml and TERRAIN_PREFERENCE_UPDATE.md for current preferences;
COMPACT_SEARCH_IMPLEMENTATION_RESULTS.md for compact benchmark evidence.
Older reports are historical and may describe superseded sizes/preferences.
The archive carries the current source, tests and selected evidence. The text
packet embeds the most relevant files. Generated binaries, databases, credentials,
node_modules and duplicated exports are excluded. No world-generation runtime is bundled.

Review the implementation and measured tradeoffs using these files, distinguishing
presence witnesses, complete predictor inventories and actual generated evidence.

# Bedrock island seed search

## Stronghold-centered Realm search (schema 5)

Current target: **4000×4000 discovery in each dimension, 1536×1536 Overworld, 896×896 Nether**. The [compact search implementation plan](COMPACT_SEARCH_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md) is implemented; measured results and remaining prediction limits are in [COMPACT_SEARCH_IMPLEMENTATION_RESULTS.md](COMPACT_SEARCH_IMPLEMENTATION_RESULTS.md). Older benchmark results retain their original geometry. Use fresh run directories for changed computation identities.

The default `search`, `doctor`, and `benchmark` commands now use the new Realm contract. Explicit schema 3/4 configurations and existing historical checkpoints retain their legacy interfaces. New results are predictions until actual Bedrock evidence establishes otherwise.

The new universal baseline discovers Strongholds in `[-2000,2000)` on both axes and records each anchor separately. Its mandatory Overworld region and Realm border are 1536×1536, centered on that Stronghold and allowed beyond discovery bounds. Village, Trial Chamber, Ancient City and Mineshaft are mandatory, together with the anchor, a broad initial Nether Fortress and at least two of five wooded families. Missing unvalidated predictions remain pending. Savanna is neutral and caves are manual-only.

V1 finds islands with connected surrounding water; V2 finds mountain basins and accepts jagged or steep interiors. Both independently consume the same saved provisionally qualified baseline pool. The former combined variant is retired. Formation-fit status stays separate; promising outside/uncertain footprints remain recorded. Final Nether bounds are 896×896 around exact Stronghold/8, requiring Fortress and Bastion. Exact generated structure enumeration and complete natural-ground validation remain unsupported/unverified; no fully verified Realm candidate is claimed.

The active terrain preferences now require **250,000 square blocks of one connected main island**, favor about **650,000**, and permit area-equivalent diameters up to **1300 blocks**. Disconnected islets do not add to the main island's area; irregular outlines are allowed and border fit uses the sampled footprint. V2 uses an inclusive **surrounding mountains** policy: substantial elevated terrain on at least **16 of 32 directions**, at least **24 blocks** above the sampled interior, at **150–700 blocks**, with no empty angular gap above **135°**. Separate mountains, open passes, rough interiors and water interiors are allowed; strict connected-arc shape is an optional historical policy. These are nomination preferences, with actual geometry verification separate. See [terrain preference update](TERRAIN_PREFERENCE_UPDATE.md). Existing results retain their old policy and need a fresh matching computation identity for further work.

```sh
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search compatibility --output runs/compatibility.json
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search baseline --count 1000 --output runs/my-realm
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search status --run runs/my-realm
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search inventory --run runs/my-realm --limit 20
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search inventory --run runs/my-realm --candidate='<seed>:<sx>:<sz>' --all-starts
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search resume --run runs/my-realm --workers 24
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search terrain --run runs/my-realm --variant all --limit 1
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search results --run runs/my-realm --variant v2 --export
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search verify-terrain --run runs/my-realm --variant v2 --top 1
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search verify-biomes --run runs/my-realm --candidate='<seed>:<sx>:<sz>' --points points.json
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search verify-realm --run runs/my-realm --candidate='<seed>:<sx>:<sz>'
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search verify-caves --seed 12345 --stronghold-x 0 --stronghold-z 0 --provider predictor --output runs/manual-caves.json
```

Use `--config config/realm.yaml` before the command for the explicit profile. Above 1000 inputs, scans need `--prediction-scale`; the persisted compatibility report records that scale is prediction-only. Benchmarks explicitly exercise this mode. `rescore --run <run> --policy <profile.yaml>` changes recorded bonus/score weights without world-generation queries. An interrupted revision resumes with `rescore --run <run> --resume`, using its persisted policy; status remains available while ranking/generation are guarded. JSONL, CSV and readable summaries export every candidate pair, independent of shortlist limits. Export sets publish atomically through `results/current`; resolve that generation once when reading multiple files.

The default eligibility pass records one viable structure-start witness where presence is enough, and visits biome points in small batches until two wooded families are found. It retains incomplete scores and durable inventory tasks. `inventory` completes family-presence bonuses and the canonical biome sample; it does not imply complete start counts or actual generation. `--all-starts --candidate` saves a separate complete bounded predictor start audit. Terrain and verification nominations complete their candidate's scoring inventory before use. `search.inventory_mode: complete` remains available for complete baseline scans.

Biome sampling uses a versioned world-aligned lattice, caches exact full-seed points, and preserves in-border positive witnesses across overlapping borders. A bounded 128-point refinement can resolve additional wooded positives; misses remain pending. Nether and End samples are supplemental; End biome IDs are predictor classes, with actual Bedrock names unverified. All 22 exposed regional/start structure families are available, including buried treasure and the correctly initialized End dimension. Monster rooms and special terrain/decorator features still require separate support.

Docker runs only for explicitly selected verification requests, including optional random low-ranking audits to detect predictor misses. It does not run per baseline seed or automatically check cave diversity. Use `verify-terrain --top N --borderline N --unusual N --random N` to select observations under the persisted budget. The pinned runtime is BDS 1.26.52.3; the native MC_26_50 predictor is separately labeled approximate. The initial verifier limits one server and 16 tiles per request; larger footprints return partial status until their budget is explicitly expanded. Verified-generation ground masking, full-sized formation controls and rare-event recall remain gates, not inferred from the small extraction tests.

Verification skips cached observations by default. `--include-cached` explicitly reviews them without charging new-attempt slots; `--retry-failed` retries failures within the remaining budget. `calibration --run <run>` reports independent V1/V2 formation diagnostics only when complete actual geometry evidence is available. Partial results never become negative truth, and selected-cohort metrics are not population accuracy estimates.

Terrain work uses a fixed saved membership revision for both variants and durable center/resolution/halo queues. Repeated limited invocations advance a rotating pool cursor, retain lower-ranked and failed work, and reuse exact prediction rasters across variants. `--pool-revision pool-00000001` selects a saved membership; `--new-pool-revision` explicitly includes later baseline arrivals. Automatic halo expansion has a resource cap; `--continue-deferred-halos` permits one additional queued expansion per center. Status separates queued pending/deferred units from pool members whose work has not been initialized. Raw terrain storage cost and retention limits still need measurement and policy.

`verify-biomes` explicitly loads up to 16 points in one BDS world. `points.json` is an array such as `[[100,200],[-300,400]]`; every point must be inside the candidate's Stronghold-centered Realm. Stable observed names can establish two wooded families, while absent or unsupported samples stay unknown. Actual surface evidence and bonus intervals are saved separately from prediction scores and exposed in exports. This command never certifies the required structures, exhaustive biome coverage, or whole terrain geometry, and never invokes cave scanning.

Measured performance and exact supported/unverified scope are in [PERFORMANCE_BASELINE.md](PERFORMANCE_BASELINE.md), [REALM_IMPLEMENTATION_RESULTS.md](REALM_IMPLEMENTATION_RESULTS.md) and `runs/realm-implementation-2026-10-10/`.

The completed [master implementation](MASTER_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md) adds mandatory final-square Fortress/Bastion prediction checks, exact eight-Y noise reuse and native quart deduplication. `config/realm.yaml` selects 18 OpenMP threads for its single-process terrain scheduler running alone; baseline remains 30 processes with one native/numerical thread each. Concurrent discovery/terrain/BDS needs a separately measured smaller profile. The code defaults retain serial compatibility. `terrain.scheduler: priority` enables the experimental durable 3:1 ranked/oldest dispatcher; rotation remains the measured default. Native thread counts and numerical-library limits are separate.

`native/build.py` builds a portable serial extension by default. On this Intel Mac, its optional host-only OpenMP build is:

```sh
ARCHFLAGS='-arch x86_64' SEED_SEARCH_OPENMP_PREFIX="$PWD/runs/master-implementation-2026-10-10/openmp/install" .venv/bin/python native/build.py
```

Keep that local runtime prefix while using the OpenMP extension. Its library hash is included in computation identity. Rebuild serial before moving to a machine without that runtime; unsupported multi-thread requests fail explicitly. No AVX ISA flag is shipped by default.

The bounded lower32 experiment has its own checkpoint and explicit raw-only ranking policy:

```sh
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search.realm.raw scan --count 4096 --threads 4 --output runs/raw-diagnostic
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search.realm.raw siblings --selection runs/raw-diagnostic/raw-selection.json --limit 128 --output runs/raw-siblings
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search raw-scan --count 4096 --mode presence --threads 18 --output runs/raw-presence
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search raw-scan --count 4096 --mode capped --ranking geometry --output runs/raw-geometry
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search raw-adaptive --selection runs/raw-geometry/geometry-selection.json --budget 4096 --output runs/adaptive-siblings
```

Use `--resume` with the original range/selection to finish interrupted chunks. Chunk/thread sizes may change while the selection identity stays fixed. Raw counts cover conservative envelopes for all permitted Stronghold centers; they cannot establish joint-center feasibility or generated-world absence. Full64 siblings pass the same required-structure baseline before terrain/verification. Scanner and terrain writes check physical workspace growth/free space as well as the existing logical raster cap. BDS checks host evidence and Docker filesystem headroom before generation. No automatic evidence deletion is performed.

Raw `presence`, `capped`, `complete`, and `reference` modes have different explicit contracts. Only capped/reference counts support the legacy density score. Geometry ranking and adaptive 1/4/16 sibling rounds are bounded experiments with an exploration reserve; independent full64 remains the default because the held-out CPU comparison did not meet the adoption gate. A full32 sweep is not justified by native throughput alone.

## Historical schema 3/4 profiles

The following sections document the preserved earlier profiles and their results. Their boundaries, categories, and defaults apply when an explicit historical configuration is supplied.

> **8 October implementation:** 2304 × 2304 Overworld / 928 × 928 Nether; four categories; mandatory Stronghold and Fortress; separate weighted biome bonus; bounded lower32/upper32 mode. Implementation and bounded verification are complete. Reference search is recommended after the two-key comparison; staged search remains available. The older sweep package is historical. No production sweep has started.

Local full signed-64-bit search for **Bedrock 26.52**, explicitly mapped to the pinned Bedrock fork's **MC_26_50** model. Matching-version Chunkbase is the external prediction reference. Actual Minecraft observations remain separate and optional. The Overworld square is 2304 × 2304; the Nether square is 928 × 928 at the Overworld center divided exactly by eight.

The pipeline searches structures-only locations, enclosed inner islands, ocean islands, and dry flat mountain basins. Bastion is optional and contributes at most two of the ten Nether proximity points. The default fast mode screens joint Stronghold/Fortress center windows before expensive terrain work. Missing predictions and incompatible nomination envelopes are saved in `stronghold-deferred.json` and `stronghold-deferred-seeds.txt`; they are not proof of absence. Recover those cases in a new bounded rank run with `config/review-all.yaml`. Known Stronghold misses mean fast mode can overlook worthy worlds. It then refines density coast connectivity, scores terrain and structures, ranks distinct seeds, and checkpoints completed indices in SQLite. This is a bounded heuristic search, not exhaustive coverage. The pinned density model excludes caves, aquifers, decoration and actual chunk interpolation; woodland is a surface-biome proxy.

Large sweeps require a readiness record matching configuration, source/native identity and verified evidence artifacts. Changing `large_search_enabled` cannot open the gate. Work status and finite pilot budgets are in [PROGRESS.md](PROGRESS.md); current results and commands are in [IMPLEMENTATION_RESULTS.md](IMPLEMENTATION_RESULTS.md) and [SWEEP_COMMANDS.md](SWEEP_COMMANDS.md). The production sweep remains unstarted.

## Commands

From this project directory, with the existing `.venv`:

```sh
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search doctor
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search regression --output runs/sweep-readiness/regression
.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search search --count 10 --workers 4 --output runs/new-pilot
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search rank --seeds seeds.txt --output runs/new-ranking
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search resume --run runs/new-pilot
```

Specify `--config path.yaml` **before** the command. Automatic enumeration applies the SplitMix64 finalizer to `(index + run_key) mod 2^64`; indices never wrap in a run. Supplied seeds are one signed decimal integer per line. JSON/CSV/SQLite seeds are decimal text. Worker scheduling does not affect predictions or ranking.

`search --dry-run` validates the finite workload and readiness gate without starting it. `--stop-after N` saves a partial bounded run for resume verification. Resume checks the original configuration, complete backend/source identity and seed sequence; incompatible resumes fail. Each completed seed is committed independently, so interrupted or out-of-order work is rerun without skipping an unfinished index.

Outputs include all center metrics and explicit rejection/deferred reasons, separate numeric rankings and interval review lists, and shortlist folders with `candidate.json`, Overworld/Nether PNGs and `inspection.md`. Required disputed raw starts remain available for Chunkbase review; they cannot be presented as website-confirmed eligible first. Marker/start border membership is separate from accessible structure footprints.

Optimization details and controlled measurements are in [FILTER_OPTIMIZATION.md](FILTER_OPTIMIZATION.md). Raw MT structure layouts alone can share lower32-bit cache keys; full seeds still determine biome/terrain/viability, and the seed sequence is unchanged. The prior readiness records do not validate this changed algorithm; use the current command and record in [SWEEP_READINESS.md](SWEEP_READINESS.md).

Fresh actual website reviews can be recorded in the schema implemented by `review.py`, then imported:

```sh
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search review --run runs/new-pilot --evidence path/to/fresh-review.json
```

`chunkbase_checked` never becomes an actual-game observation. [Frozen external regression](fixtures/bedrock-26.52/chunkbase-accuracy-audit.json) contains 159 coordinate and 880 structure rows. Six structure discrepancies remain explicitly reviewable. Exact local baseline comparisons detect new drift even within per-type empirical marker conventions. Capped positive lists do not establish complete recall or a false-positive rate.

Diagnostic `benchmark`, `probe` and actual-phone `compare` commands remain available. Their historical artifacts are preserved; stage microbenchmarks alone do not establish end-to-end throughput. The user-skipped Overworld point is not requested again.

## Native build and provenance

The supported install/rebuild path is:

```sh
.venv/bin/python -m pip install --require-hashes -r requirements.lock
.venv/bin/python -m pip install --no-build-isolation --no-deps -e .
.venv/bin/python -m seed_search doctor
```

The dependency lock targets Intel macOS/Python 3.13. The vendored Bedrock backend commit is `294c72ab0392dd818a91d293dbd5774d4874d64b`. [vendor/backend.json](vendor/backend.json) records 33 source/license hashes. Compile flags are `-O3 -std=c11 -fwrapv`, without fast-math. The direct `native/build.py` compilation entry point now changes to the repository root before compiling; its standalone CFFI target is `_native.abi3.so`, matching the Python loader. Prefer the supported editable-install path.

Current implementation verification artifacts are under `runs/implementation-2026-10-08`; `runs/filter-optimization` contains historical measurements; earlier sweep/expansion/preflight/phone/audit evidence remains preserved. [SEARCH_TARGETS.md](SEARCH_TARGETS.md) defines distances and eligibility; [CHUNKBASE_WORKFLOW.md](CHUNKBASE_WORKFLOW.md) defines external review; [GOAL.md](GOAL.md) records the implementation goal and history.

## Sweep verification

The prior `runs/filter-optimization/readiness.json` is incompatible with the new geometry and prediction identity. Final tests, two run keys with 256 inputs per search mode, three 20-seed quart comparisons, worker/resume tests and new-border external reviews are being sealed under `runs/implementation-2026-10-08`. Do not reuse the historical 2000-seed command. [SWEEP_READINESS.md](SWEEP_READINESS.md) tracks the gate.

## Enumeration and biome ranking

`full_64_bit_reference` is the default. `lower32_staged` examines 10,000 distinct raw low32 layouts, keeps 48 by raw cluster proximity plus 16 deterministic exploration selections, and evaluates four unique high32 siblings per layout. Raw placement is shared only for eight audited families. Strongholds, Trial Chambers, Mineshafts, terrain, biomes and final viability are evaluated for each complete seed. The strategy is biased; per-category useful yield must justify any default promotion.

All four categories keep the same mandatory structures. Structures-only retains a base score out of 30; terrain categories retain a base out of 100. A separate sampled resource-family bonus adds at most five points. Surface, Nether and unmeasured cave coverage are labeled separately; biome variety never repairs failed eligibility. Weights and sampling are documented in [BIOME_DIVERSITY_PLAN.md](BIOME_DIVERSITY_PLAN.md). Exact quart reuse shares raw-land booleans only; height and biome sample coordinates remain independent.

## Current landscape and structure policy

`search.example.yaml` enables four categories. The three terrain types share a finite eight-refinement budget; structures-only has its own two-center budget and skips terrain qualification. `enclosed_island` preserves separation, surrounding water/mountains and external woodland; `ocean_island` requires predicted separation plus majority ocean biomes and density water over a 1400-square window and counts woodland on the island; `mountain_basin` checks a 192-block-wide dry/gentle core and a mountain ring within 800 blocks that is connected to the floor without an inner island/moat. Defaults are tunable preferences, with finite raster/sampling uncertainty exposed. Each type has a separate distinct-seed shortlist.

Fortress and Stronghold markers must be eligible inside their respective exact squares. Missing local Strongholds remain unresolved because the enumerator is incomplete. A missing Bastion cannot veto a candidate. Website review must include Stronghold and Fortress; required checks depend on landscape type. Nothing promotes website evidence to actual Minecraft evidence.

A bounded 64 MiB exact per-seed cache separates seeds, operations and height strides. Analysis persists the sampled candidate terrain under `terrain-cache`; report generation verifies its identity and content hash before reuse. Completed-map cache reuse is invalidated by prediction/configuration/renderer/review changes. The original baseline configuration is retained at `runs/sweep-readiness/config/sweep.yaml` for regression.

# Larger islands and inclusive surrounding mountains

11 October 2026. Discovery remains 4000×4000, Overworld 1536×1536 and Nether 896×896. Required structures and wooded families retain their existing eligibility contract.

## Main-island area

Area is the sampled land area of the **single connected component containing the terrain center**. Other disconnected islands are excluded. Connected lobes count together; diagonal contacts are conservatively treated as possible land bridges. Neither area nor the equivalent diameter assumes a circular outline. Equivalent diameter is only `2*sqrt(area/pi)`; border fit uses the actual sampled formation footprint.

The former minimum equivalent diameter of 250 already implied approximately 49,087 square blocks, making the old 12,000-area floor redundant. The former 750 maximum limited island area to approximately 441,786 square blocks.

The active profile now uses:

| Preference | Value |
|---|---:|
| Minimum connected main-island land area | 250,000 square blocks |
| Favored land area in ranking | 650,000 square blocks |
| Equivalent diameter range | 500–1300 blocks; the area minimum effectively raises the lower end to about 564 |
| Favored equivalent diameter | 800–1100 blocks |
| Surrounding water | Existing 24/32 connected-water directions and 48-block threshold |

The maximum equivalent diameter permits approximately 1.33 million square blocks. Irregular islands still must fit their sampled footprint and water belt into the Stronghold-centered Realm. The 250,000 minimum is a working preference chosen after explaining the area semantics; it can be raised independently of circularity.

## Surrounding mountains

The active `enclosure_policy: surrounding_mountains` accepts substantial elevated terrain around an interior without demanding one continuous ridge, a particular arc aspect ratio, a perfect ring, a dry floor, or a flat floor.

At least 16 of 32 directions need support 24 blocks above the sampled interior, at distances 150–700 blocks. No unrepresented angular gap may exceed 12 directions (135°). Support still needs at least 32 blocks of sampled ray width and 4096 square blocks of raised component area to exclude tiny spikes. Higher relief and more surrounding directions improve ranking. Separate mountains, passes, water interiors and jagged interiors can qualify.

The existing `continuous_arcs` policy stays selectable, and saved configurations without `enclosure_policy` use that historical policy. Arc metrics remain recorded under both policies; their shape thresholds do not reject candidates in the inclusive policy. The low-level historical fallback constants are retained for explicit historical controls; `default_config()` and `config/realm.yaml` select the new active preferences.

Partial coverage, unknown ridge extents and outside-border formations retain their evidence status. Broader acceptance is a search preference change, not newly established actual-world accuracy. Current-source/config identity guards prevent silently resuming old computations with the new policy.

## Additional structures and lower32

A deterministic pilot sampled **2048 distinct dispersed lower32 layouts**. It added raw Monument, Outpost, Shipwreck and Ruined Portal presence to the existing Village/Ancient City/Fortress/Bastion/initial-Fortress requirements. This is diagnostic only; additional structures are not newly mandatory.

| Raw candidate presence domain | Base passing layouts | Passing with all four extras | Additional exclusions |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Conservative envelope covering all permitted centers | 1965 | 1965 | 0 |
| Arbitrary fixed origin center with final 1536/896 squares | 1522 | 1484 | 38 |

All four extra families had raw candidates in every conservative envelope. At the fixed origin center, only Outpost added exclusions: 38 of 2048 total layouts (1.86 percentage points), or 2.50% of base survivors. Raw candidate presence is much less selective than actual biome-valid structures. The fixed origin is not an observed Stronghold; its failures cannot reject an entire lower32 family with other possible centers.

More mandatory structures can improve selectivity when their placements genuinely exclude layouts or constrain a common center. Optional bonuses alone do not create necessary rejection conditions. This pilot did not test joint feasible-center intersections for all extras, upper32 biome viability, actual Stronghold positions, sibling allocation advantage or verified-world yield. A useful next selection experiment would measure added **joint-center/proximity** constraints against independent full64 useful yield, rather than assume more raw names deliver 99.9% rejection.

Reproduction: `.venv/bin/python scripts/pilot_additional_raw_structures.py --count 2048 --output <fresh-output.json>`.

Verification: **680 tests passed in 101.43 seconds**. The focused geometry suite passed 77 tests; verification selection/planning and the new preference controls passed 35 tests. Explicit CLI configuration and default active preferences agree, and whitespace checks pass. The verification tests now exercise the larger island coverage request and new borderline thresholds. Historical arc and small-island controls retain explicit historical preferences.

Evidence: [additional raw-family pilot](runs/terrain-preferences-2026-10-11/additional-raw-structures.json), [acceptance controls](runs/terrain-preferences-2026-10-11/acceptance-controls.json), [verification summary](runs/terrain-preferences-2026-10-11/verification.json), [full test output](runs/terrain-preferences-2026-10-11/full-tests.txt). Synthetic controls cover large irregular islands, disconnected archipelagos, bridges, separate mountains, rough interiors, open passes, one-sided mountains, tiny spikes and unknown coverage; they do not establish natural-world acceptance rates. Larger island analysis/verification halos add downstream work; no new sustained terrain/BDS throughput measurement is claimed.

FILE: COMPACT_SEARCH_IMPLEMENTATION_RESULTS.md

# Compact search implementation results

10 October 2026. Geometry remains **4000×4000 discovery, 1536×1536 Overworld and 896×896 Nether**, with exact fractional Stronghold/8 centers. The native filters, deferred inventory, stable biome sampling, recovery integration and bounded sibling allocator are implemented. **Independent full64 remains the default.** No new million-input or full32 sweep ran.

## Accuracy contract

A first-start observation uses the same pinned placement and viability rules as a complete scan and stops at the first positive. It establishes bounded **predictor presence**, not a total count, full footprint, a remnant crossing the border from an outside start, or actual Bedrock generation. Misses remain PENDING. The complete native start API and `inventory --all-starts --candidate` remain available. Stronghold discovery is unchanged and retains its known recall limitations; the existing Ancient City/Bastion fixture disagreements remain disclosed.

The 64-seed, four-boundary audit made **1,281,088 comparisons with zero differences**, including 5,376 first-witness tests across 21 families (Stronghold stays fully enumerated). All 22 exposed structure families had positive cases. Every one of the **66 IDs marked generating by the pinned model** was observed. The other 28 named IDs are not marked currently generating; this is a catalog distinction, not an absence claim. End predictions use a correct End generator; old End raw-start coordinates were checked separately from corrected viability.

Frozen-lattice qualification, pending reasons and bounds matched across both 1000-input cohorts (3,807 anchor/completion checks; zero differences). Completing 64 sampled inventories reproduced the reference's canonical biome counts, first coordinates and original bonuses. Newly supported buried treasure deliberately contributes its configured bonus. 95,256 point comparisons matched at 1/4/18 native threads. The final End optimization added 201,870 scalar/batch comparisons against the saved correct pre-optimization End adapter, with zero differences.

Scoring inventory COMPLETE means predictor family presence and a complete **defined canonical biome sample**. Individual first-witness records still say remaining starts are unqueried. Supplemental witnesses and refined points have separate provenance and never inflate canonical frequency counts. No actual-world or exhaustive biome coverage claim is made. Monster rooms, End gateways and terrain/decorator features (ravines, lava lakes, geodes and desert wells) lack a reviewed bounded adapter and remain explicitly unsupported.

## Implemented changes

- Mineshaft first-witness API with exact +8 chunk-center bounds and reference RNG; complete scans use an exact portable four-lane MT kernel with scalar fallback.
- Other regional families use first viable starts when once-per-family presence suffices. Shared Fortress/Bastion placement avoids duplicated complementary draws. Audited regular-region halos are trimmed; Stronghold halos remain.
- Wooded eligibility visits dispersed 16-point batches and stops at two families. Exact full-seed point caching, world-aligned grids, retained in-border witnesses and bounded 128-point refinement prevent the known moving-border witness loss. NULL climate hints preserve biome tie behavior.
- Correct End generator and End biome-name classes; dense End queries share one bounded height map. Supplemental Nether/End inventories stop when all active model classes have witnesses, retaining unresolved misses.
- Transactional inventory tasks have fenced leases, recovery and atomic patches. Partial scores retain uncertainty. Terrain and final verification complete scoring inventories before use; generation-free rescoring and exports preserve partial status.
- Persistence avoids repeated whole-batch JSON decoding/encoding and streams a versioned content digest. Old digest/checkpoint semantics remain guarded.
- Native lower32 presence, capped-score and complete modes have separate contracts. Batched raw selection and a versioned exploration permutation remove per-row Python ranking work. Geometry ranking remains experimental; adaptive siblings use durable 1/4/16 rounds and an exploration reserve. Canonical full64 manifests and actual spawned-worker resume are tested.

## Persisted throughput

Three timing trials per row. Reference and frozen-lattice eligibility trials were counterbalanced on identical manifests. Each cohort has 1000 distinct full64 inputs; timing repeats add evaluations, not new independent seeds. Baseline timing includes process startup and SQLite persistence, and excludes deferred inventory, terrain and BDS.

| Workload | Inputs/sec median | Worker CPU sec/1000 | Biome points | Predicted anchors | Distinct qualifying seeds | Disk MiB |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| final-reference-border-tuning | 407.1 | 7.560 | 209,088 | 337 | 281 | 16.48 |
| final-eligibility-border-tuning | 624.2 | 2.357 | 25,806 | 337 | 281 | 18.48 |
| final-eligibility-world-tuning | 611.8 | 2.681 | 30,167 | 335 | 279 | 18.70 |
| final-reference-border-holdout | 402.1 | 7.693 | 213,120 | 359 | 285 | 16.91 |
| final-eligibility-border-holdout | 614.3 | 2.201 | 17,727 | 359 | 285 | 18.71 |
| final-eligibility-world-holdout | 603.0 | 2.394 | 19,623 | 359 | 285 | 18.77 |
| final-complete-border | 377.7 | 12.279 | 208,900 | 337 | 281 | 29.24 |
| final-schedule-9x4 | 596.5 | 1.930 | 30,167 | 335 | 279 | 18.70 |
| final-schedule-2x18 | 325.9 | 1.793 | 30,167 | 335 | 279 | 18.68 |

The complete profile includes newly supported buried-treasure enumeration and fuller sampling provenance; it measured 378 inputs/sec and did not outperform the old complete reference. First-witness speedups do not make exhaustive inventory free. The active world-grid holdout is approximately 1.50× the reference; the exact frozen-lattice pass is the one that met the ≥1.5× decision-preserving gate.

Exact-contract persisted speedup: **1.53× tuning, 1.53× holdout**. The ≥1.5× gate is met. Active world-grid/refinement changes are separately versioned: 6 tuning and 2 holdout anchors changed qualification; details are in completion-verification.json. The schedule with the best bounded wall throughput remains the selected default; 30×1 avoids multiplying process/native thread counts beyond 36.

The older million-input result, 173.37 inputs/sec, used 5000/2688/1024 geometry and is not the denominator of this compact optimization speedup. These final measurements are bounded pilots, not a new massive benchmark.

### Filtering stages

Rates below are calls divided by summed worker service seconds on the median active holdout trial. They describe serial service work, not additional independent seeds/sec at each gate. Later stages receive only surviving anchors.

| Stage | Calls | Summed worker sec | Calls/service sec |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| stronghold | 1000 | 0.4215 | 2,372 |
| initial_fortress | 1000 | 0.0376 | 26,604 |
| final_nether_pair | 1908 | 0.0974 | 19,590 |
| ancient_city | 1243 | 0.2468 | 5,036 |
| village | 381 | 0.0387 | 9,839 |
| trial_chamber | 370 | 0.0137 | 27,025 |
| mineshaft | 370 | 0.0556 | 6,660 |
| wooded_biomes | 370 | 0.9923 | 373 |

Expanded scoring inventory (22 supported families plus unsupported monster-room record and supplemental dimensions): sampled median **14.34 ms/anchor**, CPU 14.34 ms/anchor, separately paid after eligibility. It is a sample, not all deferred tasks completed. The durable terrain follow-up completed 48 inventory tasks for 24 sampled anchors. Deferred cost has not vanished; completing every pending anchor would add work. Peak sampled process-tree RSS was 1.54 GiB, plus the configured 8 GiB Docker reserve, below the 25/30 GiB target/ceiling. RSS may double-count shared pages.

## Structure compute audit

Median milliseconds in the 1536-sized control square, 64 seeds including negatives. Reference is a direct old C call; current full/witness values include Python wrapper overhead, so ratios are local scan measurements rather than pipeline speedups. End comparison is raw starts versus corrected viable scans, not an old viable-scan speed ratio.

| Family | Reference full ms | Current full ms | First witness ms |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| trial_chamber | 0.0849 | 0.1086 | 0.0194 |
| stronghold | 0.2654 | 0.2897 | unchanged full enumeration |
| mineshaft | 6.0112 | 3.3139 | 0.1262 |
| ancient_city | 0.1843 | 0.1816 | 0.1403 |
| village | 0.1198 | 0.1188 | 0.0545 |
| ruined_portal | 0.0245 | 0.0258 | 0.0089 |
| shipwreck | 1.0701 | 1.1516 | 0.3309 |
| monument | 0.1199 | 0.1271 | 0.1010 |
| outpost | 0.0265 | 0.0277 | 0.0149 |
| fortress | 0.0276 | 0.0255 | 0.0090 |
| bastion | 0.0381 | 0.0391 | 0.0095 |
| desert_pyramid | 0.0871 | 0.0845 | 0.0686 |
| jungle_temple | 0.0893 | 0.0864 | 0.0676 |
| swamp_hut | 0.0890 | 0.0850 | 0.0694 |
| igloo | 0.0900 | 0.0865 | 0.0683 |
| ocean_ruin | 0.2409 | 0.2367 | 0.0615 |
| mansion | 0.3264 | 0.3252 | 0.3110 |
| buried_treasure | 3.8895 | 3.9081 | 0.2721 |
| trail_ruins | 0.0668 | 0.0746 | 0.0457 |
| abandoned_camp | 0.0460 | 0.0599 | 0.0249 |
| ruined_portal_nether | 0.0246 | 0.0332 | 0.0059 |
| end_city | 0.0431 | 0.0938 | 0.0301 |

Mineshaft fell from 6.01 ms to 0.126 ms (~48×) in this control; complete vector scans took 3.31 ms (~1.81×). Buried treasure was another major outlier (3.91→0.272 ms, ~14×): dense candidate regions and repeated biome checks caused the cost. Shipwrecks (1.15→0.331 ms, ~3.5×) and ocean ruins (0.237→0.061 ms, ~3.9×) also benefited. Shipwreck/monument viability retains its necessary nearby-biome/structure tests. Rare negative mansions gained little; accuracy checks were not removed.

OW biome classes share climate/noise work rather than having a separate expensive algorithm per biome. Most scalar OW calls were about 8 μs. End scalar calls were a separate outlier, about 17–35 μs, because of repeated height maps; the final dense 3969-point End batch was **6.7× faster**. Minimal supplemental witnesses further reduce the query count. The pinned End planar Voronoi helper is preserved exactly; the Overworld 3D nearest-cell rule must not replace it.

## Lower32 / upper32 result

The exact mode benchmark checked 4,718,592 layout-mode comparisons over 262,144 distinct dispersed layouts, at 18 and 36 threads, three counterbalanced trials. Complete counts equal reference; capped values equal min(count,32); presence equals count>0. **3.895%** failed the necessary predicate. The original expectation of 99.9% lossless rejection is not supported by this geometry.

| Mode / implementation | Layouts/sec | Full32 forecast min |
|---|---:|---:|
| Native reference, 36 threads | 49,966 | 1432.6 |
| Native complete, 36 threads | 73,364 | 975.7 |
| Native capped, 36 threads | 348,692 | 205.3 |
| Native presence, 36 threads | 3,006,603 | 23.8 |
| Old persisted presence selection | 143,794 | 497.8 |
| Old persisted capped selection | 104,165 | 687.2 |
| Final persisted presence selection | 1,442,378 | 49.6 |
| Final persisted capped selection | 299,081 | 239.3 |

Forecasts extrapolate bounded measurements and exclude full64 siblings, inventory, terrain and BDS. The 45-minute target needs 1.591 million layouts/sec end to end. Native presence passed that compute rate; the final persisted presence median forecast of 49.6 minutes still **misses the 45-minute target**. Capped ranking remains far slower. Batched selection improved persisted presence about 10× and capped ranking about 2.9×. These forecasts do not justify a full32 ranking sweep.

Village raw presence is guaranteed at every permitted center at widths ≥944; Ancient City at ≥624. Both are universal at 1536, so their old capped ranking contributes a constant 64. Fortress/Bastion placements are complementary and common in the envelope; mere presence does not prove co-location at an actual Stronghold. Counts cannot resolve full64 biome viability, wooded families, Trial placement, Stronghold recall or terrain. Upper32 siblings change those unresolved properties, so a promising low32 is a family of possibilities rather than a known good world. Mineshaft starts are common as well; adding an expensive raw Mineshaft veto offers little selectivity.

The four-arm experiment used two disjoint 4096-layout pools, three allocation keys and at most 256 selected layouts / 4096 full64 inputs per arm. It performed 34,110 evaluations of 30,231 distinct full64 seeds. Adaptive rounds used about 1400 inputs/arm and did not force expenditure of the maximum. Entire raw/feature CPU was charged to each ranked arm/key without amortization. CPU prefixes apportion parent work per input; they are analytical budget truncations of completed bounded runs, not live CPU stops.

| Held-out allocation key | Uniform / independent anchors | Legacy / independent anchors | Geometry / independent anchors |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| allocation-0 | 0.895× | 0.844× | 0.000× |
| allocation-1 | 0.887× | 0.678× | 0.000× |
| allocation-2 | 0.867× | 0.717× | 0.000× |

These are matched measured CPU-prefix yield ratios. Geometry feature construction alone exhausted the smallest holdout CPU budgets, hence zero evaluated siblings at those prefixes; it did find richer layouts when more CPU was spent. The layout-cluster bootstrap intervals and complete yield curves are in selection/experiment.json. No arm met the ≥20% heldout advantage with an interval excluding no advantage. More reuse could amortize feature cost, but that benefit has not been demonstrated. Independent full64 is retained.

All four arms received the same two-anchor/eight-refinement V1/V2 budget for each of three heldout keys (24 anchors total). Counts below are distinct anchors with any predicted geometry pass, not verified formations; many durable refinement tasks remain pending.

| Arm | Sampled anchors | Anchors with V1 geometry pass | Anchors with V2 geometry pass |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| independent | 6 | 1 | 3 |
| geometry | 6 | 1 | 3 |
| uniform | 6 | 1 | 1 |
| legacy | 6 | 0 | 0 |

This sample cannot demonstrate terrain noninferiority or verified-world advantage. No new BDS worlds were generated: the CPU adoption gate failed and no fully verified nomination was established. Generated-world advantage remains unmeasured. The deliberately interrupted serial follow-up is retained separately and excluded from the matched sample.

## Verification and evidence

Final verification: **658 tests passed in 98.87 seconds**, native ASan/UBSan PASS, exact decision/completion and End/thread comparisons PASS, doctor and CLI baseline/inventory/full-start/rescore/export/raw/sibling/adaptive smoke checks PASS, and git diff --check PASS. Frozen source/native/config integrity checked 113 unchanged files; 11 linker intermediate .o files were intentionally not archived. Timing manifests retain their measured identities. The later explicit Stronghold full-start audit fix was exercised by the final tests and fresh CLI run; the cheap eligibility path is unchanged. Sanitizer checks use the serial path; Apple leak detection is disabled and the run is not an exhaustive race/leak proof. New computational identities reject incompatible resumes. Historical runs and the frozen reference are preserved.
